## Document 054: 2026年10月FOMC会议点评：10月FOMC：静待中期选举结果

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月FOMC会议点评：10月FOMC：静待中期选举结果.pdf
Report source: 东吴证券
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告·宏观报告·宏观点评

东吴证券研究所

2026年10月29日

相关研究  
《美国中期选举：决战前夕的四个关
键问题》 
2026-10-28 
《五个维度观察权益资产超跌信号》 
2026-10-26

[Table_Tag]    [Table_Summary] 
◼ 核心观点：10月FOMC会议以10:2维持政策利率不变，发布会上沃
什并未正面回应10月为何不加息，但结合其发言大致可归纳出不加息
的一些支撑依据：①9月已撤出部分货币宽松，其“药效”仍有待观察；
②当前指标不错，市场隐含通胀预期稳定；③下一阶段信息更丰富，呼
应声明中所述的需积累更多信息（等待中期选举的结果、中东局势的变
化、AI发展的走向） 。不过，由于沃什的这番发言“含糊其辞”，2-10年
美债利差一度走高10bps至33bps。向前看，美国核心CPI环比连续2
个月超市场一致预期，鉴于Higher for Longer的油价已经带来通胀扩
散风险，今年12月的剩余1次加息料为基准情形。而2027年的利率路
径仍将取决于中期选举结果对美国通胀和增长的影响。 
◼ FOMC声明：10:2维持利率不变。10月FOMC会议美联储以10-2投
票维持政策利率在[3.75, 4]%不变，符合市场与分析师的预期。其中美联
储最鹰派票委，克利夫兰联储主席Hammack和正被特朗普重启调查的
美联储理事Cook投票加息25bps。本次FOMC会议前市场对10月美
联储加息预期已不足10%，而发布会上沃什强调当前金融条件不够紧，
但对10月不加息的“含糊其辞”仍被市场解读为鸽派信号。导致美债
利率&美元指数走低，黄金走高，不过程度相对温和。截至最新，交易
员定价12月加息概率84%，明年3月累计加息1.8次，9月累计加息
2.8次。 
◼ 发布会：对10月不加息的决定“含糊其辞”。与9月比，10月FOMC
声明一方面将近期经济活动的描述从稳固（solid）上调至强劲（strong），
另一方面认为需要积累更多信息 帮助联储决策，以确保通胀的及时
（timely）回落。发布会上沃什则主要讨论了三个问题：议息会议改革、
回应10月不加息的决定、对利率影响AI的看法。 
◼ 具体来看：①联储改革方面，沃什表示，2027年将有1次不议息的“闭
门会议”，用以专注讨论货币政策战略，2028年将提升至2次。议息会
议次数的减少意味着政策决策更长的时滞，或对应资本市场更大的风险
溢价。②利率路径方面，沃什并未正面回应10月为何不加息，但结合
其发言中大致可归纳出不加息的一些支撑依据：9月已撤出部分货币宽
松其效果仍有待观察；当前经济指标表现良好，市场隐含通胀预期稳定；
下一阶段信息更丰富，呼应声明中所述的需积累更多信息，即等待中期
选举的结果、中东局势的变化、AI发展的走向。不过，由于沃什的这番
发言“含糊其辞”，2-10年美债利差一度走高10bps至33bps。③利率影
响方面，沃什描述了近期部分科技企业信用利差的走阔，表示投资者对
AI前景存在分歧。 
◼ 展望与策略：12月加息料为基准情形，2027年利率路径仍取决于中期
选举结果。我们认为，美联储10月加息的暂缓一方面受近期市场AI叙
事降温影响，另一方面受中选政治压力与衍生出的不确定性影响。向前
看，美国核心CPI环比连续2个月超市场一致预期（详见报告《美国9
月CPI：风险在油价的Higher for Longer——2026年9月美国CPI数据
点评》 ） ，鉴于Higher for Longer的油价已经带来通胀扩散风险，今年12
月的剩余1次加息料为基准情形。而2027年的利率路径取决于届时通
胀与增长的变化，而这两者均与中选结果息息相关：①国防支出结构变
化→中东&俄乌局势→油价→通胀；②美国财政的边际变化→增长；③
AI产业监管政策→增长（详见报告《美国中期选举观战指南》）。正如10
月 FOMC声明与发布会所言，当前美联储仍需积累更多信息，而下一
阶段信息将更加丰富。我们认为随着中期选举的尘埃落地，政策上的不
确定性逐步消解，当前相对混沌的市场交易可能才会重新“拨云见日”。 
◼ 风险提示：中东局势发展超预期；美联储维持高利率水平时间过长，引
发金融系统流动性危机；通胀下行速率不及预期。

的预期（A 股市场基准为沪深 300 指数，香港市场基准为恒生指数，美国市场基准为标普

让标的），北交所基准指数为北证 50指数），具体如下： 
公司投资评级： 
买入：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在 15%以上； 
增持：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于 5%与 15%之间； 
中性：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-5%与 5%之间； 
减持：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-15%与-5%之间； 
卖出：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在-15%以下。 
行业投资评级： 
增持： 预期未来 6个月内，行业指数相对强于基准 5%以上； 
中性： 预期未来 6个月内，行业指数相对基准-5%与 5%； 
减持： 预期未来 6个月内，行业指数相对弱于基准 5%以上。

## Document 085: 2408南亞科 宏遠

Source PDF: all report pdf/2408南亞科 宏遠.pdf
Report source: Unknown

### Page 1

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

1 
2408 南亞科(Nanya Technology)

報告日期： 2026/10/13 研 究 員：翁浩軒  (Davy Wong)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 毛利率優於預期且將維持 8 成以上 
 2027 年 DRAM 供需仍緊俏 
 新廠 2027 年第四季加入貢獻

一. 3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 25.4 元 
南亞科 3Q26 營收 1336.49 億(QoQ+61.9% 、YoY+611.7%) ，其中位元
出貨量季減 4~6% ， 平均銷售單價季增超過 60%(主要因反應長期合
約重新簽訂之調整)，匯率影響有利 1~3%，3Q26 毛利率上升至
85.7%，主要因平均銷售單價上升，營業費用季增 28 億元，因當
期及未來的員工薪酬增加，稅後淨利 876.27 億(QoQ+74.58%、
YoY+5505.16%)，EPS 25.4 元。

二. 4Q26 整體產業仍將維持供不應求態勢  
受惠於 AI 需求 ， AI 運算（GPU 、 TPU 、 CPU 及 ASIC）成為 DRAM 
需求成長主力，帶動高容量、高頻寬及客製化記憶體架構需求，
範疇從 HBM 、LPDDR5 進一步擴展至網路、儲存與系統管理等領
域。在傳統應用市場方面，由於 DRAM 供給不足與 ASP 上漲，
部分傳統應用終端出現產品降規或延長生命週期的現象，低階市
場則有回收 DRAM 支撐部分需求。供給端方面，因新廠建置、設
備交期、製程導入均需要較長的前置時間，目前供給仍維持緊俏，
智慧型手機、個人電腦及一般消費性記憶體供給受限，製造商與
供應鏈端的庫存水位普遍偏低 ， 隨著供應商依 LTA 長約進行產品
組合與產能規劃，有利未來供需穩定度。

三. 新廠預估 4Q27 開始投產 
公司 5A 新廠預計 2027 年第一季裝機，2027 年第四季開始投
產 ， 預計 2028 年底前達到每月投片量 3 萬片 ， 全產能目標於 2030 
年底前達每月 5 萬片，未來產能擴充均以客戶長期合約的 AI 專
案為主要目標。另外公司亦佈局先進封測領域，規劃於屏東科學
園區建置 3D 晶圓封裝與測試廠 ， 預估 2H28 設備進駐 。 公司 2026
年前三季累計資本支出為 276 億元 ， 2026 年資本支出以不超過 697
億元為上限，其中生產設備資本支出約占五成。

四.  2026 / 2027 年獲利創高，預估 EPS 77.1 元/ 134.3 元 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：670 元 
       5 X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：527.0 元 
     3.92 X 2027 EPS 
     2026/10/12 收盤

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

2 
展望 2026 下半年至 2027 年，AI 需求維持強勁，AI 及一般雲端伺
服器 DRAM 需求持續擴大 ， 接下來逐步擴散至非雲端市場 ， 小型
語言模型推廣 AI 至邊緣裝置，新增客製化 DRAM 需求，整體記
憶體需求持續上揚。供給方面，2026 年產業微幅增加產能，預估
2027 年新增產能有限，多種 DRAM 產品將持續缺貨，三大原廠新
增產能實際貢獻要到 2028 年才會顯現 。 南亞科新廠預計 2028 年放
量，初期每月投片量 3 萬片，全產能將達 5 萬片/月。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 670 元 
南亞科為全球前五大 DRAM 廠，受惠於 AI 帶動之供給端改善，記
憶體產品漲價快速，南亞科今明年獲利創高，2028 年全球雖然有
新產能開出，但隨著 AI 由模型訓練與推理延伸至更多客製化邊緣
AI 應用，帶動記憶體位元需求長期成長。考量到 AI 應用滲透率提
升帶動記憶體需求，記憶體已成戰略物資，客戶亦開始簽訂長約，
參考 2027 年 EPS 給予 5 倍本益比，目標價 670 元，建議買進

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
南亞科成立於 1 995 年，為 DRAM （動態隨機存取記憶體）之研
發、設計、製造與銷售商，為全球第四大 DRAM 廠，市佔率約 
3%，由美光授權製程技術，其十二吋晶圓廠位於泰山南林園區，
月產能 6.8 萬片 ， 其中 20nm 製程約 3.8 萬片 ， 30nm 製程約 3 萬
片，公司產品廣泛應用於手機、PC、消費性電子產品，主要競爭
對手包括 SK Hynix、三星電子、美光、華邦電等。

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. AI 帶動記憶體價格雙位數上漲 
全球 CSP 大廠持續加強投資 AI server 及相關基礎建設，全球九大
CSP 廠之資本支出持續高速成長，2026 年資本支出將達 9220 億元
(YoY+98%)，2027 年資本支出成長至 13830 億美元(YoY+50%)。
TrendForce 預估 2026 年 HBM 需求量年增 70%。而隨著 AI 應用由
LLM 模型訓練延伸至推論，CSP 廠擴大通用型伺服器布建，也使
記憶體拉貨重心由 HBM 延伸至 RDIMM ， 進一步推升 DDR5 價格。
3Q26 在 AI 與資料中心建置需求持續下記憶體仍嚴重供不應求 ， 儘
管價格基期已高，但預估 DRAM 合約價持續上漲，TrendForce 預
估第三季整體 conventional DRAM 合約價上漲 13-18% ， 第四季持續
上漲 10%~15%。供給方面，DRAM 三大原廠今年新增產能有限，
三星及海力士均以既有廠區調整及製程轉換為主，預估晶圓產能
分別增加 5%及 8%，而美光產能持平，三大原廠新廠產能開出並

公司研究報告

3 
明顯貢獻預估在 2028 年 。 預估 2026 年 DRAM 價格逐季調升 ， 2027
年新增供給有限下，價格易漲難跌。

2. 2027 年記憶體需求維持強勁 
展望 2026 第四季至 2027 年，AI 需求維持強勁，AI 及一般雲端伺
服器 DRAM 需求持續擴大，接下來逐步擴散至非雲端市場 (如: 
Edge、客製化)，小型語言模型推廣 AI 至邊緣裝置 (PC、手機、汽
車、機器人等)，新增客製化 DRAM 需求，整體記憶體需求持續
上揚。需求面依各應用別而言，Server 方面，AI 及高效能運算持
續驅動 HBM 與 DDR5、LPDDR5 需求，高階 e-SSD、BMC、NIC
對 DDR4、LPDDR4 及 DDR5 需求顯著; PC 及手機方面，高階 PC
與手機對 DDR5 及 LPDDR5 需求維持穩定 ， 然中低階需求疲弱; 消
費型電子市場方面，AI 及一般伺服器對 HBM 、RDIMM 需求持續
擴張，排擠一般消費型記憶體供給。供給方面，2026 年產業微幅
增加產能，聚焦於高階雲端與高附加價值產品需求，DRAM 大廠
優先分配產能於 DDR5 及 HBM 產品 ， 預估 2027 年新增產能有限，
多種 DRAM 產品將持續缺貨，三大原廠新增產能實際貢獻要到
2028 年才會顯現 。 南亞科第二代 10 奈米級製程(1B) 16Gb DDR5 產
品交運量已達 10%，第三代 10 奈米級(1C)及第四代 10 奈米級(1D)
製程技術試產中。公司 DDR5 營收約占 10%，並可視需求彈性調
整增加，同時持續供應 DDR4 及 LPDDR4 產品以緩解市場缺口。
公司亦積極與策略夥伴合作拓展 AI 邊緣運算驅動的記憶體市場，
目前處工程驗證階段，預計於 2026 年底推出新產品。南亞科 2025
年資本支出 134 億元，2026 年資本支出預計不超過 697 億元，其
中生產設備支出約占五成，新廠預計於 2027 年第一季開始裝機，
2027 年第四季開始生產，2028 年放量，初期每月投片量 3 萬片，
預估 2030 年全產能將達 5 萬片/月。南亞科 2025 年位元銷售量成
長 50 多%，預估 2026 年成長 17~19%，預估 2027 年成長中低個位
數。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

1、3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 25.4 元 
南亞科 3Q26 營收 1336.49 億(QoQ+61.9% 、YoY+611.7%) ，其中位元
出貨量季減 4~6% ， 平均銷售單價季增超過 60%(主要因反應長期合
約重新簽訂之調整)，匯率影響有利 1~3%，3Q26 毛利率上升至
85.7%，主要因平均銷售單價上升，營業費用季增 28 億元，因當
期及未來的員工薪酬增加，稅後淨利 876.27 億(QoQ+74.58%、
YoY+5505.16%)，EPS 25.4 元。

公司簡介 
京元電為國內 IC 測試大廠，主要提供前段
晶圓測試(CP)及後段 IC 成品測試(FT)、預
燒等服務 ， 近年透過自製測試設備增加取得
AI 客戶信任，同時降低封裝業務來改善公
司平均獲利能力 。2Q26 營收產品組合為晶
圓測試 29.6%、產品測試 61%、產品預燒
7.7%、封裝 1.3%、其他 0.4%。

AMD(Xilinx)、意法半導體 
主要競爭對手：日月光、矽格

王彥鈞 stanley.wang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：電話訪談

京元電子 (2449 TT) 
狼來了，狼還沒來

永豐觀點 
下修 4Q26 營運展望，以反映持續的來料不順。 
 投資評價與建議 
目標價維持 320 元：京元電短期逆風未平，然 2027 年將迎來強勁成長，係考量
(1) 年底為 CPU、網通客戶等增加的產能，可自 1Q27 起完整貢獻營收，(2) 京元
電有機會增加在主要客戶 TPU 的市占率，目前已在和客戶討論下世代晶片搭配的
預燒爐，(3) HPC 新品放量，採用液冷預燒爐帶動測試單價提升。惟須留意產能快
速擴張下的成本增加。評價面，以2027 年修正後 EPS 推算目前 PER 約 23.3x，考
量 AI/HPC 相關需求未見軟弱，建議拉回再行佈局，目標價 320 元(25.2x 2027 
EPS(F))。

ESG 評析 
京元電企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向
平均得分分別為 B+等與 B+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2023 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學)

專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。 
註 2：永豐投顧已獲 SASB 授權，於研究中使用 SASB 重大性地圖之一般議題分類及揭露主題。

京元電為國內 IC 測試大廠：成立於 1987 年，主要提供前段晶圓測試(CP)及後段
IC 成品測試(FT)、預燒等服務，近年透過自製測試設備增加取得 AI 客戶信任，同
時降低封裝業務來改善公司平均獲利能力。 2Q26 營收產品組合為晶圓測試
29.6%、產品測試 61%、產品預燒 7.7%、封裝 1.3%、其他 0.4%。應用別營收比
重為消費性電子 30.3%、通訊 17.5%、數據處理與儲存 36.3%、汽車電子 13.0%、
工業 2.5%、其他 0.4%。目前主要生產據點位於桃園楊梅、苗栗竹南、苗栗頭份、
苗栗銅鑼廠區，並正在新加坡、美國等地進行佈局。主要客戶包含全球主要半導體
公司，如聯發科、韋爾半導體、AMD(Xilinx)、意法半導體等，主要競爭對手為日
月光、矽格等。 
美國廠放量或在 2028 年後 ：研究部預期台積電美國先進封裝廠區將於 2H28-2029
年進入量產，京元電尚未公布美國廠的具體時程與選址，但我們認為 AeternZ 廠
址存在落腳德州的可能性，理由有三：(1) 下游組裝廠已在當地佈局，(2) 亞利桑
那州須與晶圓廠爭搶技術人力，(3) 京元電位處後段測試，靠近終端組裝較符合供
應鏈配置。另外，本次公告的客戶預付款，我們認為宣示意義大於實質挹注，係因
封測廠資本支出以設備為大宗 ， 而該客戶的測試設備多已採託管(consignment)模
式，京元電本身需投入的設備資金不高，預付款對降低資本支出壓力的效果有限。
此外 ，京元電赴美設廠短期內本就以服務少數關鍵客戶為主 ， 此公告屬預期內的進
展，我們不建議過度解讀。 
來 料 不 如 預 期 ， 下 修 短 期 展 望 ：研 究 部 下修 京 元 電4Q26 營收至 131 億
(+8.4%QoQ，+32.0%YoY， 原估143 億)， 主要考量(1) 上游來料不順 ， 造成AI/HPC
相關產品放量幅度不如預期，而對應的 CPU 出貨則穩定墊高，(2) 客戶下修非旗
艦機款封測需求。獲利部分同樣因營收放大不如預期、新產能開出，抵銷掉少了夏
季電費的正向影響，預估毛利率 39.2%，稅後淨利 29.8 億(+14.9%QoQ，
+32.8%YoY)，EPS 2.32 元。 
2027 年展新機：目前我們並未見到 AI/HPC 終端需求轉弱的跡象，因此京元電業
績拉升僅是時間問題，預期 2027 年受惠(1) 年底為 CPU、網通等客戶增加的產
能，可自 1Q27 起完整貢獻營收，(2) 京元電有機會增加在主要客戶 TPU 的市站
率，目前已在和客戶討論下世代晶片搭配的預燒爐，(3) HPC 新品放量，採用液冷
預燒爐帶動測試單價提升。從供給端來看，楊梅廠、頭份一廠、頭份二廠的無塵室
在 1H27 陸續開出後，我們認為2027 年京元電的成長將不再受到產能限制，而是
看客戶的拉貨節奏。 
財務預估：研究部預估京元電 2026 年營收 466 億(+33.5%YoY)，毛利率 39.1%，
稅後淨利 103.5 億(-6.0%YoY，年減主因 2025 年公司處分蘇州廠房)，EPS 8.07
元(原估 8.81 元)。2027 年營收 682 億(+46.3%YoY)，毛利率 41.1%，稅後淨利
163.2 億(+57.6%YoY)，EPS 12.72 元(原估 13.93 元)。

營收走勢圖

研究員/王建智 MICHAELWANG82121188@UNI-
PSG.COM

研究部對於長榮看法轉趨正向，係因：(1) 3Q26 受惠貨櫃航運
旺季及運價回升，平均運價漲幅明顯高於燃油成本增幅， 帶動獲
利顯著成長。(2) 4Q26 雖進入傳統淡季，惟北美農曆春節前備
貨 、歐洲貨量成長及亞洲區域航線旺季提供支撐，預期獲利雖低
於 3Q26，但仍優於 2Q26。(3) 2027 年雖面臨新船交付增加的供
給壓力， 但全球碼頭擴建速度落後船隊成長，加上塞港及紅海繞
航持續消耗有效運力，有助減緩新增供給對運價的衝擊， 北美及
歐洲航線長約運價亦預期高於 2026 年，支撐全年獲利。預估
2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 分別為45.21 元（+42.7%YoY）／27.89 元
（-38.3%YoY）。歷史本淨比區間為 0.4～1.8 倍，以 2027 年底
預估每股淨值 298.36 元為評價基礎，給予0.95 倍本淨比， 目標
價 283 元，將投資評等調升至買進。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 運價漲幅高於燃油成本增幅，帶動獲利顯著成長 
長榮 3Q26 營收 1,467.76 億元，+39.6%QoQ/+51.4%YoY，主要受
惠第三季貨櫃航運傳統旺季， 歐美航線進入零售商備貨高峰， 帶
動貨運需求回升，加上紅海繞航、 全球港口壅塞持續消耗有效運
力，以及航商透過艙位控管調節供給，推升主要航線運價。7、8
月平均運費分別為 1,539 及1,577 美元／TEU， 參考9 月SCFI 月
均 3,657 點，+8.0%MoM， 推估9 月平均運費約 1,703 美元／TEU，
並假設 9 月貨量與 8 月持平，則 3Q26 平均運費約 1,606 美元／
TEU，+44.7%QoQ，貨量276 萬 TEU，-1.8%QoQ，營收成長主要由
運價上漲貢獻。

成本方面 ， 預估每噸耗油成本由2Q26 的 645 美元升至 714 美元 ，
+10.6%QoQ，假設季度耗油量持平，燃油費用增加約22 億元，惟
運價漲幅明顯高於燃油成本增幅 ， 營收增加帶來的獲利貢獻可望
大幅超過成本上升影響。預估 3Q26 稅後淨利 479.69 億元，
+199.2%QoQ/+120.6%YoY，稅後EPS 22.16 元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

圖一、各月平均運費、每噸耗油成本、貨量

資料來源：長榮，統一投顧整理 
2. 4Q26 進入傳統淡季，惟區域航線需求支撐營運 
第四季為貨櫃航運傳統淡季， 整體需求不如第三季旺季強勁， 惟
根據訂艙及裝載情況 ， 至11 月前貨量仍具支撐 。 北美航線方面 ，
美國主要港口進口貨櫃量維持高檔，零售庫存銷售比低於 1.3，
顯示零售商庫存水位相對偏低，仍有持續補貨需求，預期 11 月
貨量隨淡季趨緩 ，12 月則有農曆春節前備貨需求支撐 。 歐洲航線
方面 ，2026 年貨量成長優於預期 ， 雖運價自7 月後逐步回落 ， 但
紅海地緣政治風險尚未解除， 繞航仍持續消耗有效運力。 亞洲區
域航線第四季進入傳統旺季 ， 受惠中國加一帶動東南亞及南亞原
料、半成品運輸需求成長。此外，全球塞港造成約300 萬至 400
萬 TEU 有效運力損失，占全球總運力約 11%至 12%，搭配航商艙
位控管，有助支撐淡季運價。

預估 4Q26 營收 1,174.21 億元，-20.0%QoQ/+37.0%YoY，稅後淨
利 255.68 億元，-46.7%QoQ/+200.2%YoY，稅後 EPS 11.81 元。

3. 2027 年新船交付壓力增加，港口壅塞限制有效運力供給 
2027~2029 年為全球貨櫃船新船交付高峰，目前全球貨櫃船總運
力約3,400 萬TEU，至2030 年待交付新船運力超過1,400 萬TEU，
主流研調機構(Clarksons、Drewry)預估 2027 年名目運力供給增
速為 7.7~7.9%，供給成長壓力仍是 2027 年主要風險。惟名目運
力增加不代表有效運力等幅成長 ， 過去十年全球碼頭處理能力年
均成長約 2.7%，低於船隊運力年均成長約 5.4%，港口基礎設施
擴張速度落後船隊成長，塞港問題短期難以改善。紅海及蘇伊士
運河恢復通行亦為重要變數， 歐洲航線若恢復通行，單程約節省

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

7 天 、 往返約14 天 ， 但船舶集中抵港可能加劇碼頭壅塞 ， 抵銷部
分航程縮短釋出的運力。長榮截至 2030 年尚有 72 艘新船待交
付 ， 合計運力約89 萬 TEU， 並持續投資重要樞紐碼頭 ， 提升靠泊
效率及船隊調度彈性。

4. 2027 年歐美長約運價預期高於 2026 年，支撐全年獲利 
2027 年北美及歐洲航線長約運價預期均高於 2026 年，主要受惠
三項因素 。 首先 ，2026 年第三季貨櫃運價大幅上漲 ， 目前現貨運
價明顯高於前一年度長約議價時的水準 ， 為新年度合約價格調升
奠定基礎 。 其次 ， 全球港口處理能力擴張速度落後船隊運力成長 ，
加上紅海繞航及主要港口壅塞持續消耗有效運力 ， 航商供給調節
能力提升，有利維持長約議價能力。最後，歐美航線貨運需求仍
具支撐 ， 北美零售商庫存水位偏低 ， 歐洲航線貨量成長優於預期 ，
提供長約價格上調條件。歐洲航線新約自 2027 年初開始適用，
北美航線則自 5 月起生效 ， 預期新年度長約運價調升將逐步反映
於營收，並有助減緩現貨運價波動對獲利的影響，支撐 2027 年
營運表現。

5. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 45.21/27.89 元，塞港及長約運
價調升支撐獲利高於歷史平均水準 
預估 2026/2027 年營收分別為 4,558.69／4,178.70 億元，分別
+20.3%YoY/-8.3%YoY，稅後淨利分別為 978.74／603.81 億元，
分別+42.7%YoY/-38.3%YoY，稅後 EPS 分別為 45.21 元／27.89
元。2026 年受惠第三季運價大幅上漲 ， 帶動全年獲利回升 。2027
年雖面臨新船交付增加及運價自高檔回落的壓力 ， 惟全球碼頭處
理能力成長落後船隊運力，加上塞港及紅海繞航限制有效供給，
歐美航線長約價格亦預期高於 2026 年 ， 有助減緩獲利下滑幅度 。

Update
Unimicron  | Asia Pacific
Key ABF substrate customer
announces capacity reservation
agreements
What's new? According to  a press release on October 21, at GTC Berlin NVIDIA
(covered by Joseph Moore) named Unimicron as one of  three partners in its plan to
lift substrate capacity 2.5x over three years. A capacity reservation agreement
supports Unimicron’s KF (Kuangfu) plant expansion in Taiwan, with additions
expected from 2Q27 and full capacity by mid-2028.

Our read-through: We believe this implies robust multi-year, AI-driven substrate
demand. NVIDIA’s capacity reservation likely covers Unimicron's 2Q27 KF Plant 2
step flagged in July (see our earnings call takeaways), which could lower the
financial risk of overcapacity as Unimicron deploys its NT$53.7bn 2026 capex
budget. Stay OW with a price target of NT$1,775.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Unimicron (3037.TW)
Base case, residual income (RI) valuation model, which we think derives the most accurate
value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. We use a cost of equity of 9.2%
[risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield), equity risk premium of 8.7%, and
a beta of 1.0], a medium-term growth rate of 14%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected ABF substrate demand from PC and server customers

n Capex cuts; halt to its capacity expansion plan
n Continued yield issues of alternative technology that doesn't require substrate (e.g.,
CoWoP)
Risks to Downside
n Sudden demand shortfall
n Technological change that would not require ABF substrates
n Intensifying competition
n Yield issues or production hiccups when ramping new capacity

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 123: 261027_daiwa_unimicron

Source PDF: all report pdf/261027_daiwa_unimicron.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Our first take on Unimicron’s 3Q26 results (released on 27 October) is that 
earnings were better than expected, driven by stronger-than-expected non-operating 
gains. We attribute the upside primarily to a smaller-than-feared valuation loss on UMC in 
3Q26. In addition, the company mentioned that other investments, including Unimicron 
shares held through investment vehicles, contributed c.TWD3bn of mark-to-market gains in 
3Q26. As for the core business, 3Q26 revenue reached TWD52.7bn (up 23% QoQ, up 
55% YoY), with gross margin expanding 4.0ppt QoQ to 28.8% and operating margin rising 
4.5ppt QoQ to 20.0%, supported by improving profitability in ABF substrates and HDI. We 
believe the profitability performance was broadly in line with the latest buy-side 
expectations. Looking ahead, while the company does not provide numerical guidance, 
revenue and gross margin should continue to improve in 4Q26. Given Unimicron’s leading 
position across most CSP AI ASIC projects, Nvidia GPUs, and EMIB-T, we believe the 
company should continue to benefit from a favourable pricing environment amid the 
current supply-demand dynamics and ongoing specification upgrades, including larger 
substrate body sizes and technology migration. We have a Buy (1) rating on Unimicron 
with a 12M TP of TWD1,500, based on a target PER of 35x applied to our 1-year-forward 
EPS forecast. Key downside risk: weaker-than-expected demand.

Unimicron’s 3Q26 results highlights  
(TWDm) Actual QoQ% YoY% Daiwa Diff % Consensus Diff % 
Revenue 52,650 23% 55% 51,251 3% 50,366 5% 
Gross profit 15,189 43% 234% 14,513 5% 14,044 8% 
  Gross margin (%) 28.8%   28.3%  27.9%  
Operating profit 10,528 58% 587% 9,901 6% 9,878 7% 
  Operating margin (%) 20.0%   19.3%  19.6%  
Pre-tax profit 13,163 -15% 347% 5,473 141% 8,577 53% 
Net profit 10,052 -23% 358% 4,184 140% 6,738 49% 
EPS (TWD) 6.15 -23% 358% 2.72 140% 4.12 49% 
Source: Company data, Daiwa forecasts, Bloomberg

What’s the impact: 
Below is our first take on Unimicron’s 3Q26 results (released on 27 October). We will 
review our model in due course.

• 3Q26 results beat expectations. Unimicron’s 3Q26 earnings came in at TWD10.1bn 
(EPS: TWD6.15), beating our estimate of TWD4.2bn and Bloomberg consensus of 
TWD6.7bn by 140% and 49%, respectively, mainly due to higher-than-expected non-
operating gains. In our previous note, Unimicron recognised c.TWD8bn of mark-to-
market gains on UMC (2303 TT, TWD122.5, Neutral [3], covered by Rick Hsu) in 2Q26. 
We had originally expected Unimicron to recognise c.TWD5bn of valuation losses in 
3Q26, given UMC’s share-price performance in July. However, the actual valuation loss 
of c.TWD0.7bn was substantially better than feared. In addition, the company attributed 
part of the strength to c.TWD3bn of other mark-to-market gains, including gains related 
to Unimicron shares held through its investment vehicles. This is broadly consistent with 
the reported 3Q26 total non-operating contribution of c.TWD2.6bn after accounting for 
the UMC valuation loss and other non-operating items.

Country

27 October 2026

MEMO 
 3Q26 results first-take: results beat on non-ops

Unimicron Technology Corp (3037 TT)

Sheng Cheng

### Page 2

Unimicron Technology Corp (3037 TT) 
27 October 2026 
2

• Looking at the core business, 3Q26 revenue reached TWD52.7bn (up 23% QoQ, up 
55% YoY), with gross margin expanding 4.0ppt QoQ to 28.8% and operating margin 
rising 4.5ppt QoQ to 20.0%, supported by improving profitability in ABF substrates and 
HDI. With stronger operating leverage, operating profit surged 58% QoQ to 
TWD10.53bn. We believe the gross-margin and operating-margin performance was 
broadly in line with recent buy-side expectations for modestly improving profitability in 
ABF substrates and HDI, given that ABF margin was already high at c.40% in 2Q26, 
alongside a higher revenue contribution from a large end customer. We estimate ABF 
substrate margin at c.42-44% in 3Q26 (vs. c.40% in 2Q26).

• Business outlook. While the company does not provide numerical guidance, revenue 
and gross margin should continue to improve in 4Q26 amid the current substrate supply 
shortage. In addition, the company is aggressively expanding capacity at its Guangfu 
and Yangmei plants over 2027-29, with roughly c.30% annual ABF capacity expansion, 
which should become a key growth driver for Unimicron. Given Unimicron’s leading 
position across most CSP AI ASIC projects, Nvidia GPUs, and EMIB-T, we believe the 
company should continue to benefit from a favourable pricing environment amid the 
current supply-demand dynamics and ongoing specification upgrades, including larger 
substrate body sizes and technology migration.

What we recommend: 
We have a Buy (1) rating on Unimicron with a 12M TP of TWD1,500, based on a target 
PER of 35x applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key downside risk: less-than-
expected demand.

the amount of the transaction will be in excess of the required collateral or margin requirements**.

real estate prices, commodity prices, and others. In addition, depending on the content of the transaction, the loss could exceed the amount of the collateral or margin requirements.

## Document 171: 35 岁不会写代码？这波 AI 红利，可能就轮到你了

Source PDF: all report pdf/35 岁不会写代码？这波 AI 红利，可能就轮到你了.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

客⼾现在缺的，是既懂业务怎么运转、⼜能亲⼿把  AI 从⼀个想法做成上线系统的⼈。
摘要：
数据显⽰，全球科技公司年内已宣布裁员近  20 万⼈。与此同时，有⼀个  AI 岗位的招聘量却在
⼀年⾥暴涨了  7 倍多，薪资中位数折合⼈⺠币约  130 万元。

OpenAI 、 Anthropic 、微软、亚⻢逊、⾕歌，⼏家巨头前后砸下近百亿美元，有的开新公司，有
的建新部⻔，抢的都是这类⼈才。最反常识的是：这个岗位最稀缺的本事，恰恰不是写代码。

Anthropic ：培养  1 万个 “ 把  AI 送进企业的⼈ ”

10 ⽉  2 ⽇， Claude 的开发商  Anthropic 宣布投⼊  1 亿美元成⽴ “Claude 前沿学院 ” （ Claude
Frontier Academy ），⽬标是在  2027 年底前培养  1 万名 “ 前沿部署⼯程师 ” 。

⾸批学员是从合作伙伴中选出的在职⼯程师，来⾃埃森哲、摩根⼠丹利、诺和诺德和⼏家顶级
咨询公司。培训分两段：先是  4 天线下集训，从⼀次 “ 模拟企业部署 ” 开始；考核通过后，学员回
到⾃⼰公司，带⼀个真实项⽬做  12 周实战。⾸批认证预计在  2027 年初完成。

在  Anthropic 看来，企业落地  AI 的瓶颈不在模型，⽽在于缺少能推动变⾰的⼈。负责这个项⽬
的⾼管  Steve Corfield 说，客⼾现在缺的，是既懂业务怎么运转、⼜能亲⼿把  AI 从⼀个想法做
成上线系统的⼈。

这只是今年  AI 巨头抢⼈⼤战中的最新⼀例。

砸下百亿美元，巨头们都在抢同⼀种⼈才

今年  5 ⽉， OpenAI 成⽴了专⻔帮企业落地  AI 的 “OpenAI 部署公司 ” ，由  TPG 领投，⾸轮融资
超过  40 亿美元。同⽉， Anthropic 与⿊⽯、 Hellman & Friedman 、⾼盛合资成⽴了⼀家  AI 服
务公司，各⽅初始投资约  15 亿美元。

6 ⽉底到  7 ⽉初，亚⻢逊  AWS 和微软先后出⼿： AWS 投⼊  10 亿美元，组建⾃⼰的部署⼯程
团队；微软投⼊  25 亿美元成⽴新部⻔，把约  6000 名⼯程专家派驻到客⼾企业。 9 ⽉，⾕歌云
和埃森哲成⽴联合业务部⻔，计划培训最多  1000 名埃森哲⼯程师，专⻔派驻客⼾现场。

过去这些公司⽐的是谁的模型更强、参数更⼤、跑分更⾼，如今却不约⽽同地把钱砸向同⼀个
⽅向：派⼈进客⼾公司，把  AI 真正⽤起来。

这类⼈才在业内有个统⼀的叫法：FDE （ Forward Deployed Engineer ），中⽂常译作“ 前线部
署⼯程师 ”。

招聘量三年猛涨 30 倍，年薪中位数超百万元

熟悉美股的读者，对这个词的源头不会陌⽣。 FDE 最早是  Palantir （帕兰泰尔）在⼗多年前系
统化推⾏的⼀种做法：⼯程师不坐在总部写通⽤产品，⽽是常驻客⼾现场，和客⼾⽤同⼀套数
据、扛同⼀个业务指标，把模糊的需求、分散的数据和落不了地的⽬标，⼀点点变成真正能⽤
的系统。

当年很多⼈觉得这种模式太重、太慢，不像⼀家软件公司该⼲的事。今天回头看，这恰恰是
Palantir 最难被复制的护城河： 2026 年第⼆季度， Palantir 营收  19.35 亿美元，同⽐增⻓
93% ，其中美国商业收⼊同⽐增⻓  149% 。公司⾸席执⾏官  Alex Karp 有⼀个在业内被反复引⽤
的判断：⼤模型当然重要，但⾄少在未来⼏年⾥，价值在于落地实施。

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

据劳动⼒市场分析机构  Lightcast 统计， 2023 年全美  FDE 招聘⼴告不到  200 条， 2025 年约
1200 条， 2026 年仅前九个⽉就已超过  7300 条。这些⼴告标出的薪资中位数约  19.5 万美元，
⽐  2023 年⾼出  40% ，是全美所有招聘⼴告薪资中位数的三倍多。 Indeed 的数据也指向同⼀个
⽅向：美国  FDE 职位从  2025 年  4 ⽉的  643 个增⾄  2026 年  4 ⽉的  5330 个，⼀年涨了  7 倍
多。

值得注意的是，今年前九个⽉发布  FDE 招聘⼴告最多的雇主中，德勤、埃森哲等咨询公司排在
了⾕歌等科技巨头之前。可⻅  FDE 已不只是  AI 公司的内部技术岗位，任何想把  AI ⽤进业务的
企业都需要这样的⼈。

需要说明的是， FDE 不等于 “ 驻场外包 ” 。外包是甲⽅说什么就做什么；FDE 则要先判断⼀件事
该不该⽤  AI 、⽤哪个模型、花多少钱、怎么证明有效，再把它真正落地上线。它要解决的是企
业⾥的⼀个⽼难题：懂技术的不懂业务，懂业务的不懂技术。

AI 落地难在业务，稀缺的恰恰是懂业务的⼈

很多⼈看到这⾥会想：这是年轻程序员的机会，我不写代码，也过了转⾏的年纪。

但不妨看看  Anthropic 怎么描述⼀个好的  FDE ：熟悉  Agent 系统、擅⻓解决问题、善于沟通、
有⼯程经验。这四条⾥，有⼀半在技术之外。

过去两年，⼏乎每家企业都买过  AI 产品、做过  AI 试点，但真正跑进⽇常业务的远⽐宣传的
少。⼏组被业内⼴泛引⽤的数据或许能说明⼀些问题：标普全球的调查显⽰，企业  AI 项⽬的弃
⽤率⼀年内从  17% 升到了  42%； Gartner 预计，到  2027 年超过四成的  Agent 项⽬会被取消；
IDC 与联想  2025 年的⼀份报告则显⽰，88% 的  AI 概念验证项⽬最终没有投⼊⽣产。

问题往往不出在模型上 —— 演⽰时⼀切顺利，⼀进⼊真实业务，就会碰上脏数据、⽼系统、权
限审批、员⼯习惯，以及 “ 出了错谁负责 ” 。毕⻢威的⼀位⾸席技术架构师举过⼀个例⼦：⼀个应
⽤先花了⼏个⽉梳理需求和业务背景，真正⽤  AI 开发只⽤了⼤约⼀个⽉。

FDE 真正值钱的那⼀半能⼒不是编程，⽽是懂业务。⼀份合同哪⼏条要紧，⼀张报表哪个数字
会出事，⼀个审批流程卡在哪⼀步，哪件事交给机器做会让⽼板睡不着觉，这些  AI 替代不了，
反⽽是在⾏业⾥⼲了⼗⼏、⼆⼗年的⼈最清楚。

你缺少的不是经验，⽽是把经验交给  AI 去执⾏的那⼀步：知道今天的  AI 能做什么、不能做什
么、该怎么搭、要花多少钱、出了错怎么兜底。

我们这次的训练营，就是为这⼀步准备的。

两天⾛完  AI 落地最后⼀公⾥

华尔街⻅闻⾸期「 FDE 实战训练营」将于  11 ⽉  14 ⽇⾄  15 ⽇在上海开班。这是⼀个线下实战
营，限量  50 席，在两天时间⾥，带你从零做出⼀个真正能⽤、能上线的  AI Agent 。

先说说  Agent （智能体）是什么。很多⼈对  AI 的印象还停留在 “ 问⼀句答⼀句 ” 的聊天上，但
Agent 不⼀样：你交给它⼀件事，它会⾃⼰查资料、打开软件、读写⽂件、交出结果，做错了还
能⾃⼰返⼯。巨头们派  FDE 进企业，部署的正是这种技术。

两天训练营共  12 个模块，从模型怎么选、价格怎么算，⼀直讲到评测、成本优化和安全上线，
都是⼤⼚把  AI 做上线时真正在⽤的⽅法。主讲讲师  Right 曾任腾讯⾼级算法⼯程师，主导过微
信核⼼  AI Agent 项⽬的算法设计和规模化落地，在  Agent 系统设计、模型训练、 Harness 架构
⽅向有深厚积累。他还在  NeurIPS 、 ACL 等顶级会议发表过多篇  Agent ⽅向论⽂，相关⼯作被
Google DeepMind 引⽤。

课程适合谁
企业主和管理者：想弄清  AI 到底能替公司做哪些事、每件事花多少钱、⻛险在哪⾥

### Page 3

AI
想在  AI 时代给⾃⼰多留⼀项硬技能的⼈

三⼗年前，先学会⽤  Excel 的那批⼈，在后来的⼆⼗年⾥少熬了⽆数个夜。
今天巨头们花百亿美元在抢懂业务、⼜会⽤  AI 的⼈，⽽你已经有了⼀半。剩下那⼀半，就在这

FoPLP 進度維持，2027 年邏輯產品線為主要動能：公司主要營收來自 DRAM 和
NAND 封測，並耕耘高階 FCBGA 封裝、FoPLP 等技術已久，2027 年將逐步開花
結果 ， 目前公司在大尺寸晶片封裝已有拿到相關訂單 ， 客戶涵蓋AI 應用晶片業者 、
CSP 等，FoPLP 進度維持 1Q27~2Q27 放量，同樣屬邏輯產品線。公司對 2027
年記憶體市場仍持樂觀態度，表示韓廠產能沒開這麼快， 中國業者的產能則優先供
需中國本土需求仍不夠用，故供需狀況仍看不到反轉跡象。整體研究部認為 2027
年公司動能以邏輯產品為主，記憶體成長幅度相對溫和，預估 2026 年營收為
973.19 億元(+29.9%YoY)，毛利率 23.0%，營業利益率 16.6%，稅後淨利 102.31
億元 (+84.8%YoY)，稅後 EPS 13.86 元， 2027 年 營 收 為1,222.37 億元
(+25.6%YoY)，毛利率 26.3%，營業利益率 20.3%，稅後淨利 167.11 億元
(+63.3%YoY)， 稅後EPS 22.62 元 ， 維持買進建議 ， 目標價340 元(15X 2027 EPS)。

## Document 247: [034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한_07bab1f0

Source PDF: all report pdf/[034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한_07bab1f0.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(034020) 
두산에너빌리티 
2026.10.30

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 80,000원(하향) 
 현재주가 56,700 원(10/29)

 [투자의견] 당사는 두산에너빌리티에 대해 목표주가를 80,000원(2027년 
BPS의 6.2배)으로 27% 하향하며, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 실적은 견조
한 회복을 보이고 있으나, 최근 GE Vernova(GEV) 등 글로벌 AI 및 에너지 
밸류체인 Peer 주가 조정을 반영해 멀티플을 하향 조정했다. 체코 시공 계
약 등 일부 노이즈에 의해 주가가 급락한 만큼 매수 기회라고 판단한다.  
 [3Q26 Review]  매출액 4.4조원(-5.8%qoq, +14.7%yoy), 영업이익 
2,612억원(-16.9%qoq, +90.6%yoy)을 기록해 당사 추정에 부합하는 무
난한 실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티 부문은 매출액 1.8조원, 영업이익 1,211
억원(영업이익률 6.6%, +2.3%p qoq)을 기록하며 원자력·가스터빈 중심의 
고수익 사업 믹스 개선으로 분기 수익성이 재차 개선됐다. 당기순이익은 
149억원(-93.4%qoq)에 그쳤으나, 원달러 환율 급락으로 발생한 환손실 
반영 때문으로 이익 체력과는 무관하다고 판단한다. 3Q26 미국 빅테크 데
이터센터향 가스터빈 첫 출하를 달성했으며, 수주잔고는 에너빌리티 부문 
기준 26.6조원(+70.9%yoy)으로 사상 최대치를 경신했다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 4.8조원, 영업이익 2,640억원으로 유사한 실적 
흐름을 전망한다. 4Q 가스터빈 인도와 체코 두코바니 원전 진행률 반영으로 
에너빌리티 부문 외형 성장이 기대된다. 3Q26 별도 기준 영업이익률이 
8.5%까지 개선된 만큼 에너빌리티 부문의 분기 실적 개선 추세는 유효하다
고 판단한다. 연간 수주 목표 13.3조원 달성을 위한 잔여 수주(3.3조원 +α)
가 예정되어 있으며, 대형원전을 제외하더라도 가이던스 달성은 무난할 것
이라 판단한다. 세부적으로는 SMR 1조원(계약 조건 합의 완료 단계로 연내 
체결 가능), 신재생 7,000억원(해상풍력 기자재·EPC 2건 협의 완료 단계), 
해외 복합 EPC 5,000억원(국내 1건, 해외 2건)이 기대된다. 
 [가스터빈] 3Q26 누적 가스터빈 13기·스팀터빈 11기·LTSA 6건을 달성하
며 연간 10기 이상 수주·수행 역량이 안정적으로 유지되고 있다. 2030년 
슬롯 예약 물량의 본계약 전환은 2027년, 2031년 슬롯은 2028년으로 예
상되며, 수주잔고 증가가 기대된다. 가스터빈 수요 증가를 감안하면 추가 캐
파 증설 결정이 이르면 연내 이루어질 전망이다. 
 [원전] 10월 NuScale 1개 모듈 제작 계약을 체결했으며, PPA 지연 여부와 
무관하게 완결된 계약으로 실적에 반영될 전망이다. X-energy는 연내 기자
재 및 부품의 후속 계약을 추진 중이다. TerraPower 주기기 3종 중 2종 계
약이 완료되어 제작에 착수했으며, Rolls-Royce SMR·i-SMR 등 파트너십 
확대도 지속되고 있다. 이외에 대미투자 관련 기자재 발주는 미국 부지 확정 
및 한국 의회 승인 절차가 남아있어 내년 발주가 기대된다. 기존 웨스팅하우
스 AP1000 Vogtle 계약 대비 업무 범위 확대(원자로·증기발생기 외 추가 
품목) 가능성이 있어 긍정적으로 평가한다.

 발행주식수(천주) 640,561

### Page 2

두산에너빌리티

2_ Eugene Research Center 
설명회 Q&A 
Q1. 4Q26 SMR 수주 목표 약 1 조원의 실현 가능성 
- 고객사와 GTC 등 주요 계약 조건에 대한 합의를 대부분 완료하고 계약 체결 대기 중 
- 연내 계약 가능성이 있어 수주 목표는 의미 있는 수준

Q2. NuScale 모듈 1 기 제작 계약의 PP A 지연 영향 
- 완결된 제작 계약으로 PP A 체결이 지연되더라도 해당 모듈의 제작 및 진행에는 문제없음

Q3. AP1000 장주기 기자재 선발주 후 인허가 지연 시 회계 처리 및 사업 영향 
- DOE·DOC 및 한국 정부 재원을 활용해 EPC 계약·착공 지연과 무관하게 기자재 제작 가능 
- 프로젝트 중단 시 웨스팅하우스의 다른 프로젝트로 기자재 전용 가능성 검토

Q4. 체코 원전 컨소시엄 변경 시 참여 및 사업 계획 
- 현지 지점 운영과 시공인력 상주로 시공 환경 자료 확보 
- 한수원과 초기 인허가·설계에 참여하고, 주기기 공급·설치 역량을 바탕으로 경쟁력 확보 기대

Q5. 체코 원전 EPC 사업의 수익성 확보 가능성 
- 한수원 가이드에 따라 수익성을 고려한 입찰 진행 예정

Q6. 웨스팅하우스 SPV 및 폴란드 AP1000 장주기 기자재 발주 진행 상황 
- DOE·DOC 프로젝트와 폴란드 AP1000 3기 기자재 조기 발주 협의 중 
- 연내 수주 여부는 불확실하나 조기 수주 추진

Q7. 3Q26 영업이익률 개선 요인 
- 원자력·가스터빈 중심의 사업 믹스 개선이 수익성에 기여 
- 제작 진행률과 마일스톤에 따라 이익 인식

### Page 3

두산에너빌리티

Eugene Research Center _3 
Q8. 4Q26 복합화력 수주 목표 5,000억원의 달성 가능성 
- 국내 1 건, 해외 2 건 협의 중이며 국내 프로젝트의 수주 가능성이 높은 상황 
- 대미 투자 물량은 기존 2031년까지의 예약계약 납기와 비교해 검토 중

Q9. 3Q26 에너빌리티부문 당기순손실 원인 
- 급격한 환율 하락에 따른 약 800억원의 환산손실이 주요 원인

Q10. 대미 투자 관련 기자재 발주 시점 
- 미국 부지 확정, 웨스팅하우스 사업제안 및 한국 정부 심사·국회 승인 절차 필요 
- 연말~내년 초 발주 여부는 불확실하나 조기 발주 추진

Q11. 대미 투자 프로젝트의 기자재 공급 범위 확대 가능성 
- 원자로·증기발생기 외 추가 품목까지 공급 범위 확대를 위해 웨스팅하우스와 협상 중

Q12. 장주기 기자재 선발주 후 인허가 지연 시 실적 영향 
- 기자재 출하 시 수금 조건이 충족되며 선발주 자금도 사전 확보 
- 보글·VC 서머 사례에서 기자재 대금을 회수해 프로젝트 지연에 따른 실적 영향은 제한적

Q13. 4Q26 신재생·풍력 수주 목표 약 7,000억원의 달성 가능성 
- 해상풍력 기자재·EPC 계약 및 유지보수 계약 추진 중 
- 주요 계약 협의가 상당 부분 완료돼 일정 지연이 없다면 연내 목표 달성 전망

Q14. 중형 가스터빈 개발 및 해외 진출 계획 
- 대형 가스터빈은 한국·미국 중심으로 추진하며 중동 고객사와도 협의 중 
- 휴스턴 서비스 허브를 기반으로 미국 데이터센터 시장 대응 
- 중형 가스터빈은 시제품 제작 및 시험 진행 후 2028년 하반기 출하 목표

### Page 4

두산에너빌리티

4_ Eugene Research Center 
Q15. NuScale 모듈 1 기 선발주 배경 
- 대규모 일괄 발주에 대비한 제작 경험 확보 및 납기 리스크 완화를 위해 사전 제작 추진

Q16. X-energy 연내 후속 계약 대상 품목 
- 증기발생기 전열관 및 주단 소재 등 장주기 품목의 사전 발주 추진

Q17. DOE 프로젝트의 장주기 기자재 공급 범위 확대 가능성 
- 웨스팅하우스 공급망 상황에 따라 추가 품목 협상 중 
- 과거 보글·VC 서머보다 넓은 공급 범위 기대

Q18. 웨스팅하우스 지분 투자와 기자재 계약 시점의 연계 가능성 
- 지분 투자는 내년, 기자재 계약은 연내 또는 내년 초 목표로 시점이 일치할지는 불확실

Q19. X-energy 장주기 기자재의 소재·부품 구분 
- 증기발생기 전열관은 부품, 주단 소재는 소재에 해당 
- 전열관은 공급 병목에 대비해 조기 발주하고 주단 소재는 설계 확정 후 제작 준비

Q20. 복합화력 주기기 수주의 기존·신규 고객 비중 
- 기존 고객과 추가 예약계약을 협의 중이며 신규 글로벌 고객과도 예약계약 협상 진행

Q21. 주요 SMR 사업 외 파트너십 확대 가능성 
- Rolls-Royce 사업 추진 및 GE Hitachi BWRX-300 주단 소재 예약계약 체결 
- i-SMR은 2035년 운전을 목표로 2028년 이전 발주가 필요해 관련 협의 진행

Q22. X-energy 전열관·주단 소재 수주 물량 규모 
- 다우케미컬 초도 사업은 80MW 원자로 4 개 모듈로 구성되며, 해당 4 개 모듈분 부품·소재 공급

### Page 5

두산에너빌리티

Eugene Research Center _5 
Q23. 중동 지정학적 불안정에 따른 프로젝트·물류 영향 
- 기존 프로젝트의 운송비 증가분을 발주처·개발사와 협의 중 
- 신규 계약에는 비용 보상 및 공기 지연 관련 조항 반영

Q24. 가스터빈 생산능력 증설 규모 및 시기 
- 현재 생산능력 증설 중이며 2031년 출하분까지 예약 접수 
- 시장 수요에 따라 추가 증설 검토

Q25. 2030년 가스터빈 예약 물량의 실제 계약 전환 시점 
- 2030년 출하 물량은 대부분 내년 실제 계약 여부 결정 전망 
- 2031년 출하 물량은 2028년경 계약 여부 결정 예상

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%) 
ㆍSTRONG BUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +50%이상 3% 
ㆍBUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +15%이상 ~ +50%미만 94% 
ㆍHOLD(중립) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%이상 ∼ +15%미만 3% 
ㆍREDUCE(매도) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%미만 0% 
 (2026.06.30 기준)

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다 
기다렸던 4 행정 중속 엔진 캐파 증설 단행 
HD현대중공업의 4행정 엔진 캐파는 기존 3.0GW에서 증설이 완료되는 '28년부터 
램프업을 거쳐 '30년 7.2GW까지 확대될 계획이다. ①[육상 발전용]은 신공장(울산 
온산) 3GW 와 HD 현대엔진(전남 영암) 1GW 를 합쳐 4.0GW, ②[선박용]은 
2.3GW에서 3.2GW로 확대된다. HD현대엔진 캐파는 기존 0.7GW에서 1GW까
지 확대되며, 해당 라인은 신공장이 가동되기 전 ‘27~’28 년 구간에서의 선박&육상 엔
진 모두 대응 가능하다. 신공장 캐파는 총 3.0GW로, ‘28년 약 30%의 가동률로 시
작해 램프업을 거쳐 ‘30 년부터 풀캐파로 20MW급 모델을 생산할 예정이다. 4 행정 
중속엔진에 대한 수요 가시성은 높다. 동사가 공유했던 육상 발전 엔진 인콰이어리 규
모는 기존 약 3~9GW였으나, 현재는 이를 크게 상회하는 수준으로 소통하고 있다.

최근 부유식 데이터센터(FDC)에 대한 하이퍼스케일러들의 인콰이어리 또한 확대되고 
있다. HD 한국조선해양은 최근 미국 선급(ABS)로부터 100MW급 규모의 부유식 발
전 설비(BMPP)에 대한 기본 인증(AiP)를 획득했으며, 동사는 FDC 신조 사업과 더불
어 발전원 4 행정 엔진을 파워십 및 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)에 납품하며 그룹사들과 
패키지 사업으로 진행할 예정이다. 가시화된 수요를 기반으로 엔진기계사업부의 구조적 
성장 보여줄 것으로 전망하며, 이에 따른 리레이팅을 예상한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 및 목표주가 기존 97 만원 유지 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’ 및 목표주가 기존 97 만원을 유지한다. 동사는 
올해 상선수주 목표 이미 초과달성하며 안정적인 펀더멘털 유지 중이다. 하반기 남은 
기간은 LNGC, VLGC/VLAC 중심의 선별 수주 전략을 통해 ‘28 년 이후의 상선 부
문 수익성 유지 충분히 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 글로벌 지정학적 리스크 확대에 따른 
해군 현대화 사이클 기조에 맞추어 미 해군은 동맹국의 호위함 구매를 고려하고 있으
며, MASGA를 기반으로 중장기적으로 미국 전투함을 건조하기 위해 HD현대의 미국 
진출 가능성 높다는 판단이다. 또한 미국을 제외한 글로벌 함정(페루, 필리핀, 말레이시
아, 칠레, 사우디, 이집트 등) 수주 파이프라인도 다수 보유하고 있어 함정 모멘텀에 따
른 리레이팅 충분히 가능할 전망이다. 특수선+4 행정 엔진+FDC+SMR 등 조선소에 
붙일 수 있는 모든 추가 성장동력을 보유한 유일한 종목이기에 조선업 최선호주(Top-
Pick)로 제시한다. 최근 불거지고 있는 노조 파업 문제 해소될 시 저점 매수 기회라는 
판단이다.

## Document 266: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속LG에너지솔루션(373220) 원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속원-_fba73583

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속LG에너지솔루션(373220) 원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속원-_fba73583.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-21

Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026.09.21   
LG에너지솔루션 
(373220)

원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속 Company Brief

3Q26 원/달러 환율 하락에도 불구하고 영업이익 시장 기대치 상회 전망 
동사의 3Q26 실적은 매출 8.8조원(+45% YoY, + 16% QoQ), 영업이익 3,690억원(-
39% YoY, +226% QoQ)을 기록하며 원/달러 환율 하락으로 매출은 시장 기대치를 
소폭 하회하겠으나 영업이익은 상회할 것으로 전망된다. AMPC 는 미국 ESS 생산량 
증가와 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동에 힘입어 전분기 대비 약 57% 증가한 3,790억원으로 
추정된다. 미국 상호관세 위법 판결에 따른 관세 환급금이나 완성차 업체들의 최소 
구매 물량 미달 보상금 반영 여부에 따라 추가적인 이익 개선 가능성도 존재한다. ESS 
부문은 전분기에서 이연된 물량의 출하가 본격화되면서 3Q26 매출이 전분기 대비 약 
38% 증가할 것으로 예상된다. 특히 미국 ESS 생산라인의 가동 안정화와 AMPC 수혜 
확대에 힘입어 수익성도 점진적으로 개선될 것으로 보인다. 자동차전지 부문도 
3Q26부터 회복세가 강화될 것으로 예상된다. 유럽향 물량 회복과 GM 얼티엄셀즈 
재가동으로 매출이 전분기 대비 큰 폭으로 증가하고, 고정비 부담 완화로 적자폭도 
뚜렷하게 축소될 전망이다. 소형전지 부문은 주요 고객사인 Tesla의 판매 호조에 따른 
안정적인 출하 증가로 매출 회복과 수익성 방어가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 
4Q26 대규모 ESS 신규 수주 기대 
전세계 ESS 시장은 신재생 에너지 발전 확대와 AIDC 투자 본격화에 힘입어 빠르게 
성장하고 있다. 시장조사기관 SNE리서치에 따르면 1H26 전세계 ESS 배터리 
출하량은 461GWh로 전년 동기 대비 약 71% 증가했다. 이 중 북미 출하량은 
76GWh로 전년 동기 대비 약 83% 증가했으며, 분기별로는 1Q26 35GWh(+91% 
YoY), 2Q26 41GWh(+76% YoY) 를 기록했다. 통상 하반기에 출하가 집중되는 
계절성을 감안하면 올해 북미 ESS 배터리 출하량은 전년 대비 약 70% 증가한 
170~180GWh에 이를 것으로 전망된다. 연초 동사가 예상한 올해 북미 ESS 수요 
성장률 10% 초반 수준을 크게 상회할 가능성이 높은 상황이다. 올해 동사의 ESS 
신규 수주 목표는 약 90GWh이며, 현재까지 약 30GWh의 수주를 확정한 것으로 
파악된다. 이에 시장 일각에서는 연간 수주 목표 달성 여부에 대한 우려가 제기되고 
있다. 그러나 당사가 파악한 바에 따르면 대규모 ESS 신규 수주가 4Q26에 집중될 
것으로 예상된다. 특히 북미 ESS 수요의 가파른 증가세와 투자세액공제(48E)에 
연계된 PFE 공급망 규제 시행은 동사의 수주 확보에 우호적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
매수 투자의견과 목표주가 유지 
동사에 대한 매수 투자의견과 목표주가를 유지한다. 향후 국내 배터리 셀 업체들의 
북미 ESS 신규 수주가 지속적으로 확대될 전망이다. 미국 ESS 시장에서는 신재생 
에너지 발전 확대와 AIDC 투자 본격화에 따른 수요 증가, 중국 공급망에 대한 규제 
강화가 맞물리면서 국내 업체들에 우호적인 사업 환경이 조성되고 있다. 동사 역시 
ESS 중심의 생산라인 전환과 가동률 상승이 본격화될 경우 매출 성장과 함께 고정비 
부담 완화, AMPC 수혜 확대에 따른 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 이를 바탕으로 동사의 
영업이익은 2026년 약 0.8조원에서 2028년 약 5.1조원으로 가파르게 증가할 것으로 
전망된다. ESS의 역할이 신재생 에너지 연계용 저장장치를 넘어 AIDC의 안정적인 
전력 공급을 지원하는 핵심 인프라로 확대되고 있다는 점에도 주목할 필요가 있다. 
이에 따라 동사의 밸류에이션 역시 기존 EV 중심의 평가에서 벗어나 전력 인프라 
산업의 성장성을 반영하는 방향으로 재평가되어야 한다고 판단된다. 업종 내에서 실적 
개선 가시성과 정책 수혜가 뚜렷한 동사에 대한 비중 확대 전략이 유효하다. 
Buy (Maintain)

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급  
시가총액기준 산업별 시장비중대비 보유비중의 변화를 추천하는 것임 
 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report ; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 ( 1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised  to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

AUS: Sep Private Sector Credit (%mth)
Oct 30, Last: 0.6, Westpac f/c: 0.5

Private sector credit growth came in slightly firmer than
expected at 0.6%mth in August, above Westpac’s and the
market’s forecast for a 0.5%mth gain, leaving credit growth

running at 8.4%yr. Growth in housing credit continued to ease,

NZ: RBNZ Official Cash Rate Review (%)
Oct 28, Last: 2.75, Westpac f/c: 3.00, Mkt f/c: 3.00
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. Data since September show a higher
outlook for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP starting

point, but increased risks to domestic consumption and
residential investment. On balance, the medium-term inflation

NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses
were submitted in the early part of the month, while fuel prices
took a sharp step higher from the second half of September. The

survey comes too late for the RBNZ’s October policy review, but
evidence about the breadth of cost and price pressures could
have a bearing on the RBNZ’s thinking about the total amount of
policy tightening needed in the months ahead.
NZ: Sep Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Oct 28, Last: 0.2, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has been gradually ticking higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result for
September – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase. Given the
history of this measure, however, both this month and the
previous month are likely to be revised down a little from their
initial release.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.
That said, Committee members who have spoken recently
have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively
pricing out the chance of an October hike in favour of a

December move, which is also our expectation.
US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

## Document 309: Cliff Notes_ managing expectations (2026-10-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ managing expectations (2026-10-30).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:04 October 23 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’ attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor
Hauser's fireside chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a follow-up rate hike at the
November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook was "materially worse" than the RBA's

### Page 2

August forecasts. Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having "crystallised" ahead of the
September meeting – the Middle East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser noted the
Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in
August”. He then positioned AI and data centre developments among an "extraordinary sequence of
positive demand shocks around the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through into

CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy Governor noted that "inflation's been coming
in at or a little bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the labour market". He went on to
state that the staff are currently assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement on
capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other words, revise up their assumption for the
NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of market pricing for a follow-up November

hike, from just a 20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition today ahead of next week's
Q3 CPI. For that release, we are forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a 0.9 (3.5%yr)
increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3
as well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast. Provided inflation prints broadly in line
with our expectations, the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.

Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3 and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to
3.7%yr. Under the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely watched measures of core
inflation took a step higher to now be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our NZ
economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October
policy review, while retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.

Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised to the upside in Q3, GDP growth accelerating from
0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on track to
achieve their 4.5-5.0% full-year growth objective.
While annual retail sales growth improved in September, rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace
of growth is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very weak versus history. Consumer
spending continues to be constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to employment

prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five years into the property downturn, house prices continue
to decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep downturn, -20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in
high-tech investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst domestically oriented businesses
as well as property’s malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and 2028 as the

contribution from net exports moderates, broad-based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect
recent targeted support measures to be broadened and given much greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr in September, exceeding both market
expectations and the Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely driven by energy, fuel
prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services

inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an immediate concern inflation, the labour market
showing resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to 4.7% in August, suggests demand

### Page 3

Related articles
risks for inflation will need close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is
released. For both events, the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The week after, the
US mid-term elections are due.

## Document 353: Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP .  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research

### Page 2

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP , and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP , although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.

Exhibit 8: UK Growth Forecasts Have Drifted Higher

### Page 7

10. Our strongest global view is that central banks will deliver fewer hikes than currently 
priced.  In addition, our rates strategists flag underweight positioning, diminished 
technical risks, and risk asset pressures as reasons why the recent rally might extend.  
The market’s narrow breadth makes our equity strategists wary of momentum factor 
exposure, but they still expect the S&P 500 to resume its climb after the midterm 
election, as earnings continue to grow solidly.  Our credit strategists remain cautious on 
IG technology bonds but see scope for some near-term reversal in HY given the scale of 
the recent selloff.  Our FX strategists expect the Japanese yen to act as a hedge against 
further AI jitters, in part because such jitters would shrink US-Japan interest rate 
differentials.  And our commodity strategists recommend targeted exposures to oil 
products and European natural gas to hedge near-term escalation risks alongside 
longer-term exposures to copper, gold, and select power markets to benefit from 
structural supply/demand imbalances.
